On the event
\{\tau_{N}<T_{N}/2\}
,

\mu^{N}_{t-T_{N}+T_{N}\wedge\tau_{N}}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\eta}
.
Therefore, by Proposition

4.14
,
we
can replace
\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{N}
by
{\mathcal{U}}

inside the expectation
and hence get

(4.51)

\begin{split}&\Bigl|\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{N}(t,\mu)-{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[{\mathcal{U}}\Bigl(T_{N}-T_{N}\wedge\tau_{N},\mu^{N}_{t-T_{N}+T_{N}\wedge\tau_{N}}\Bigr){\mathbbm{1}}_{\{\tau_{N}<T_{N}/2\}}\big|\,\mu_{0}^{N}=\mu\Bigr]\Bigr|\leq\frac{C}{N}.\end{split}

By Proposition
4.1
,
we know that, for
s\geq T_{N}/2
and
\nu\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\eta}
,

(4.52)

\begin{split}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\nu)=\Phi\bigl({\mathcal{L}}(X_{s}|X_{0}\sim\nu)\bigr)&=\Phi({\mathcal{I}})+O\bigl(\frac{1}{N}\bigr)={\mathcal{U}}(t,\mu)+O\bigl(\frac{1}{N}\bigr),\end{split}

with the Landau symbol
{\mathcal{O}}(\cdot)
being independent of

\nu
,
from which we
deduce that we can replace the expectation
in
(
4.51
)
by
{\mathcal{U}}(t,\mu)
. This completes the proof.
In the above, we used the slightly abusive notation

{\mathcal{L}}(X_{s}|X_{0}\sim\nu)

to denote the law of
X_{s}
in (
1.3
) when
the law of
X_{0}
is
\nu
.
∎

4.5
End of the proof of Theorem
4.3

For an
N
-sample
(Y^{i,N}_{0})_{i=1,\cdots,N}

with law
{\mu_{\text{init}}}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\eta}
, for some
\eta>0
, we let

\mu_{0}^{N}=(1/N)\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{Y_{0}^{i,N}}
.

First Step.

We start with the following (quite standard) computation:

(4.53)

\begin{split}{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\|{\mu_{\text{init}}}-\mu^{N}_{0}\|_{-1,2}^{2}\Bigr]&=\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{(1+n^{2})}{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\biggl|\int_{\mathbb{T}}e^{-{\mathrm{i}}2\pi n\theta}\bigl({\mu_{\text{init}}}-\mu^{N}_{0}\bigr)(\mathrm{d}\theta)\biggr|^{2}\biggr]\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{(1+n^{2})}\leq\frac{c}{N},\end{split}

for some universal constant
c\geq 0
.
We deduce that, for any
\varrho>0
,

{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\bigl\|{\mu_{\text{init}}}-\mu^{N}_{0}\bigr\|_{-1,2}\geq\varrho\Bigr)\leq\frac{c}{\varrho^{2}N}.

Now, we choose
\varrho
such that, for any two probability measures

\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\in{\mathcal{P}}(\mathbb{T})

with

\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|_{-1,2}\leq\varrho
, it holds that

|\nu_{1}^{1}-\nu_{2}^{1}|\leq\eta/2
(we recall that

\nu_{1}^{1}
and
\nu_{2}^{1}
are the 1-Fourier modes of
\nu_{1}

and
\nu_{2}
), from which we get that, for a constant
C
depending on

\eta
,

{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\mu^{N}_{0}{\not\in}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\eta/2}\Bigr)\leq\frac{C}{N}.

Therefore, by Proposition

4.15
,
there exists a constant
C
, only depending on
\kappa
,
\eta

and
the bounds for
\Phi

in
(
Reg
-
\Phi
-(
{\alpha,2}
))
, such that

\forall t\geq 0,\quad\Bigl|{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{N}\bigl(t,\mu_{0}^{N}\bigr)\bigr]-{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}\bigl(t,\mu_{0}^{N}\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{\{\mu_{0}^{N}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\eta/2}\}}\bigr]\Bigr|\leq\frac{C}{N}.

We deduce that it suffices to show that

(4.54)

\Bigl|{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\Bigl({\mathcal{U}}(t,{\mu_{\text{init}}})-{\mathcal{U}}\bigl(t,\mu_{0}^{N}\bigr)\Bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{\{\mu_{0}^{N}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\eta/2}\}}\Bigr]\Bigr|\leq\frac{C}{N}.

Second Step.
In order to prove
(
4.54
), we may argue as in the second step of the proof of
Proposition

4.14
.
Indeed, we can consider a smooth function
\Xi:{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}})\rightarrow[0,1]
such that

\Xi
is 1 on the set
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\eta/2}
and 0 outside the set

{\mathcal{Q}}_{\eta/4}
.
Then, instead of proving
(
4.54
), it suffices to show
that

(4.55)

\Bigl|{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[{\mathcal{U}}(t,{\mu_{\text{init}}})\Xi({\mu_{\text{init}}})-{\mathcal{U}}\bigl(t,\mu_{0}^{N}\bigr)\Xi\bigl(\mu_{0}^{N}\bigr)\Bigr]\Bigr|\leq\frac{C}{N}.

Thanks to the cut-off function
\Xi
,
the function
(t,\mu)\mapsto{\mathcal{U}}(t,\mu)\Xi(\mu)
satisfies the conclusion
of Corollary

4.12
, even though
\mu\not\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\eta}
.
This suffices to
apply
(
2.8
)
with
{\mathcal{U}}(t,{\mu_{\text{init}}})\Xi({\mu_{\text{init}}})
instead of
{\mathcal{U}}(t,{\mu_{\text{init}}})
therein.
∎

The proof of Theorem
4.3
is hence completed provided that we prove
the following lemma, which we invoked in the proof of Lemma

4.15
:

Lemma 4.16
.

There exist a constant
\eta\in(0,1)
and constant
C
, both independent of
N
, such that,
for any initial distribution
\mu\in{\mathcal{P}}(\mathbb{T})

and any
t\geq 0
, the distribution of the stopping time

\tau_{N}:=\inf\{s>0:|\int_{\mathbb{T}}e^{-{\mathrm{i}}2\pi\theta}\mu^{N}_{s+t}(\mathrm{d}\theta)|\geq\eta\}

satisfies

{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(\tau_{N}\geq N^{1/4}\bigr)\leq C/N
.

Without any loss of generality, we can assume that

t=0
.

First Step.
We go back to the shape of particle system (
1.1
) with
b
as in
(
4.1
):

\begin{split}\mathrm{d}Y_{t}^{j,N}&=-\frac{2\pi\kappa}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\sin\Bigl(2\pi\bigl(Y_{t}^{j,N}-Y_{t}^{k,N}\bigr)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}t+\mathrm{d}W_{t}^{j},\quad t\geq 0.\end{split}

Let

E_{t}^{j,\ell,N}=\exp({\mathrm{i}}2\pi\ell Y_{t}^{j,N})
.
Then,
recalling the notation
\overline{z}
for denoting the complex conjugate of a complex number
z\in{\mathbb{C}}
, we obtain that

\begin{split}\mathrm{d}E_{t}^{j,\ell,N}&=-\frac{2\pi^{2}\kappa\ell}{N}E_{t}^{j,\ell,N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Bigl(E_{t}^{j,1,N}\overline{E}_{t}^{k,1,N}-\overline{E}_{t}^{j,1,N}E_{t}^{k,1,N}\Bigr)dt-2\pi^{2}\ell^{2}E_{t}^{j,\ell,N}\mathrm{d}t+{\mathrm{i}}2\pi\ell E_{t}^{j,\ell,N}\mathrm{d}W_{t}^{j}\\
&=-2\pi^{2}\kappa\ell\Bigl(E_{t}^{j,\ell+1,N}\mu_{t}^{1,N}-\overline{E}_{t}^{j,\ell-1,N}\overline{\mu}_{t}^{1,N}\Bigr)\mathrm{d}t-2\pi^{2}\ell^{2}E_{t}^{j,\ell,N}\mathrm{d}t+{\mathrm{i}}2\pi\ell E_{t}^{j,\ell,N}\mathrm{d}W_{t}^{j},\end{split}

where

\mu_{t}^{\ell,N}=N^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\overline{E}_{t}^{j,\ell,N}

is the
\ell
-Fourier mode of
\mu_{t}^{N}
.
Taking the mean over
j\in\{1,\cdots,N\}
, we get

\begin{split}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{t}^{\ell,N}&=-2\pi^{2}\kappa\ell\Bigl(\overline{\mu}_{t}^{\ell+1,N}\mu_{t}^{1,N}-\mu_{t}^{\ell-1,N}\overline{\mu}_{t}^{1,N}\Bigr)\mathrm{d}t-2\pi^{2}\ell^{2}\overline{\mu}_{t}^{\ell,N}\mathrm{d}t+{\mathrm{i}}2\pi\ell\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}E_{t}^{j,\ell,N}\mathrm{d}W_{t}^{j}.\end{split}

Choosing
\ell=1
and recalling that
\kappa>1
, we can find a constant
c>1
, only depending on
\kappa
, such that

(4.56)

\mathrm{d}\bigl[|\mu_{t}^{1,N}|^{2}\bigr]=\bigl(c^{-1}-c|\mu_{t}^{2,N}|\bigr)|\mu_{t}^{1,N}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t+\frac{c^{-1}}{{N}}\mathrm{d}t+\mathrm{d}K_{t}^{1}+\mathrm{d}M_{t}^{1},

where

(K_{t}^{1})_{t\geq 0}

is a non-decreasing absolutely continuous process and

(M_{t}^{1})_{t\geq 0}
is a martingale satisfying

[\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}t]\langle M^{1}\rangle_{t}\leq c/N
.
Similarly,
choosing
\ell=2
, we get

(4.57)

\mathrm{d}\bigl[|\mu_{t}^{2,N}|^{2}\bigr]=\bigl(c|\mu_{t}^{1,N}||\mu_{t}^{2,N}|-c^{-1}|\mu_{t}^{2,N}|^{2}\bigr)\mathrm{d}t-\mathrm{d}K_{t}^{2}+\frac{c}{{N}}\mathrm{d}t+\mathrm{d}M_{t}^{2},

where

(K_{t}^{2})_{t\geq 0}

is a non-decreasing absolutely continuous process and

(M_{t}^{2})_{t\geq 0}
is a martingale satisfying

[\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}t]\langle M^{2}\rangle_{t}\leq c/N
.
We now let

(4.58)

\Lambda_{t}^{N}=|\mu_{t}^{2,N}|^{2}-c^{4}|\mu_{t}^{1,N}|^{2},\quad t\geq 0.

Using the expansions (
4.56
)
and
(
4.57
), we obtain

(4.59)

\begin{split}\mathrm{d}\Lambda_{t}^{N}&=\Bigl[\bigl(c|\mu_{t}^{1,N}||\mu_{t}^{2,N}|-c^{-1}|\mu_{t}^{2,N}|^{2}\bigr)-c^{4}\bigl(c^{-1}-c|\mu_{t}^{2,N}|\bigr)|\mu_{t}^{1,N}|^{2}\Bigr]\mathrm{d}t-\frac{c^{3}-c}{{N}}\mathrm{d}t-\mathrm{d}K_{t}+\mathrm{d}M_{t},\end{split}

where
(K_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is a non-decreasing absolutely continuous process and

(M_{t})_{t\geq 0}

is a martingale satisfying

(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}t)\langle M\rangle_{t}\leq C(c)/N
,
in which
C(c)
is a constant that only depends on
c
.
We then consider the same stopping time

\tau_{N}

as in
the statement, but with
\eta=c^{-4}/4
.
As long as
t\leq\tau_{N}
, we have (notice that the term below is nothing but the first term in the expansion of

\mathrm{d}\Lambda_{t}^{N}
)

\begin{split}&\bigl(c|\mu_{t}^{1,N}||\mu_{t}^{2,N}|-c^{-1}|\mu_{t}^{2,N}|^{2}\bigr)-c^{4}\bigl(c^{-1}-c|\mu_{t}^{2,N}|\bigr)|\mu_{t}^{1,N}|^{2}\\
&=-c^{3}|\mu_{t}^{1,N}|^{2}-c^{-1}|\mu_{t}^{2,N}|^{2}+c|\mu_{t}^{1,N}||\mu_{t}^{2,N}|+c^{5}|\mu_{t}^{2,N}||\mu_{t}^{1,N}|^{2}\\
&\leq-c^{3}|\mu_{t}^{1,N}|^{2}-c^{-1}|\mu_{t}^{2,N}|^{2}+\frac{5}{4}c|\mu_{t}^{1,N}||\mu_{t}^{2,N}|\\
&=-\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}\Bigl(|\mu_{t}^{2,N}|^{2}+c^{4}|\mu_{t}^{1,N}|^{2}\Bigr)-\frac{5}{8}\Bigl(c^{3/2}|\mu_{t}^{1,N}|-c^{-1/2}|\mu_{t}^{2,N}|\Bigr)^{2}\leq-\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}\Lambda_{t}^{N}.\end{split}

By modifying the definition of

(K_{t})_{t\geq 0}
in (
4.59
) and by assuming that
c^{3}\geq 2c
, we then get

\begin{split}\mathrm{d}\Lambda_{t}^{N}=-\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}\Lambda_{t}^{N}\mathrm{d}t-\frac{c}{N}\mathrm{d}t-\mathrm{d}K_{t}+\mathrm{d}M_{t}.\end{split}

Therefore,

(4.60)

\begin{split}\Lambda_{t}^{N}&\leq\exp\Bigl(-\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}t\Bigr)\biggl[\Lambda_{0}^{N}+\int_{0}^{t}\exp\Bigl(\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}s\Bigr)\mathrm{d}M_{s}\biggr].\end{split}

We now observe that, for any integer
n\geq 0
,

\begin{split}&\sup_{n\leq t\leq n+1}\biggl[\exp\Bigl(-\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}t\Bigr)\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\exp\Bigl(\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}s\Bigr)\mathrm{d}M_{s}\biggr|\biggr]\leq\sup_{n\leq t\leq n+1}\biggl[\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\exp\Bigl(\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}(s-n)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}M_{s}\biggr|\biggr].\end{split}

Accordingly, by Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequalities, we deduce that, for any integer
p\geq 1
,
there exists a constant
C_{p}(c)
, depending on
p
and
c
, such that

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\sup_{n\leq t\leq n+1}\biggl[\exp\Bigl(-\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}t\Bigr)\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\exp\Bigl(\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}s\Bigr)\mathrm{d}M_{s}\biggr|^{p}\biggr]\biggr]\\
&\leq\frac{C_{p}(c)}{N^{p/2}}\biggl(\int_{n}^{n+1}\exp\Bigl(\frac{3}{4}c^{-1}\bigl(s-n)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\biggr)^{p/2}=\frac{C_{p}(c)}{N^{p/2}}\biggl(\int_{0}^{1}\exp\Bigl(\frac{3}{4}c^{-1}s\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\biggr)^{p/2}.\end{split}

In turn, by Markov’s inequality, we deduce that, for any
\varepsilon>0
,

{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\biggl\{\sup_{n\leq t\leq n+1}\biggl[\exp\Bigl(-\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}t\Bigr)\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\exp\Bigl(\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}s\Bigr)\mathrm{d}M_{s}\biggr|\biggr]\geq\varepsilon\biggr\}\biggr)\leq\frac{C_{p}(c)}{\varepsilon^{p}N^{p/2}},

for a new value of the constant
C_{p}(c)
, and then

{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\bigcup_{n=0}^{\lfloor N^{1/4}\rfloor}\biggl\{\sup_{n\leq t\leq n+1}\biggl[\exp\Bigl(-\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}t\Bigr)\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\exp\Bigl(\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}s\Bigr)\mathrm{d}M_{s}\biggr|\biggr]\geq\varepsilon\biggr\}\biggr)\leq\frac{C_{p}(c)}{\varepsilon^{p}N^{p/2-1/4}},

where
\lfloor N^{1/4}\rfloor
denotes the floor of
N^{1/4}
.
Choosing
p
large enough and using a new value of the constant
C(c)
, we end up with

{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\biggl\{\sup_{0\leq t\leq N^{1/4}}\biggl[\exp\Bigl(-\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}t\Bigr)\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\exp\Bigl(\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}s\Bigr)\mathrm{d}M_{s}\biggr|\biggr]\geq\varepsilon\biggr\}\biggr)\leq\frac{C(c)}{\varepsilon^{p}N}.

The value of
p
that appears in the right-hand side is hence fixed.
This prompts us to introduce the event

A_{N}(\varepsilon):=\biggl\{\sup_{0\leq t\leq N^{1/4}}\biggl[\exp\Bigl(-\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}t\Bigr)\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\exp\Bigl(\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}s\Bigr)\mathrm{d}M_{s}\biggr|\biggr]\geq\varepsilon\biggr\}.

On the complementary of the latter event, we have, by
(
4.60
),

\begin{split}\forall t\in\bigl[0,N^{1/4}\wedge\tau_{N}\bigr],\quad\Lambda_{t}^{N}&\leq\exp\Bigl(-\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}t\Bigr)\Lambda_{0}^{N}+\varepsilon.\end{split}

Back to the definition of (
4.58
), this yields

\forall t\in\bigl[0,N^{1/4}\wedge\tau_{N}\bigr],\quad|\mu_{t}^{2,N}|^{2}-c^{4}|\mu_{t}^{1,N}|^{2}\leq\exp\Bigl(-\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}t\Bigr)\Lambda_{0}^{N}+\varepsilon,

that is, for all

t\in[0,N^{1/4}\wedge\tau_{N}]
,

|\mu_{t}^{2,N}|^{2}\leq\exp\Bigl(-\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}t\Bigr)\Lambda_{0}^{N}+\varepsilon+c^{4}|\mu_{t}^{1,N}|^{2}\leq\exp\Bigl(-\frac{3}{8}c^{-1}t\Bigr)+\varepsilon+c^{4}|\mu_{t}^{1,N}|^{2},

where we used the obvious inequality

\Lambda_{0}^{N}\leq 1
.

Second Step.

We thus introduce the following times. First, we call
t_{0}(\varepsilon)
the smallest (deterministic) time such that

\exp(-(3/8)c^{-1}t_{0}(\varepsilon))\leq\varepsilon
. Secondly, we let

\begin{split}&\sigma_{N}(\varepsilon):=\inf\bigl\{t\geq{t_{0}(\varepsilon)}:|\mu^{1,N}_{t}|\geq\varepsilon c^{-4}\bigr\}.\end{split}

Then, assuming that
\varepsilon\in(0,1/4)
and
recalling that
\eta=c^{-4}/4
in the definition of
\tau_{N}
, we obviously have
\sigma_{N}(\varepsilon)\leq\tau_{N}
if
t_{0}(\varepsilon)\leq\tau_{N}
, which implies

|\mu_{t}^{2,N}|^{2}\leq 3\varepsilon
(as a consequence of the first step),
for all
t\in[t_{0}(\varepsilon),N^{1/4}\wedge\sigma_{N}(\varepsilon)\wedge\tau_{N}]
,
at least if the latter interval is not empty.
Subsequently, plugging the latter into
(
4.56
), we obtain, for
t
in the same interval,

\mathrm{d}\bigl[|\mu_{t}^{1,N}|^{2}\bigr]=\bigl(c^{-1}-\sqrt{3}c\varepsilon^{1/2}\bigr)|\mu_{t}^{1,N}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t+\frac{c^{-1}}{{N}}\mathrm{d}t+\mathrm{d}\widetilde{K}_{t}^{1}+\mathrm{d}M_{t}^{1},

for a new non-decreasing absolutely continuous process

(\widetilde{K}_{t}^{1})_{t\geq 0}
.

So far,
\varepsilon
has been a free parameter. Now, we can choose it such that

c^{-1}-\sqrt{3}c\varepsilon^{1/2}=c^{-1}/2
. For this given value of
\varepsilon
(which is now frozen in terms of
c
),
we get

\mathrm{d}\bigl[|\mu_{t}^{1,N}|^{2}\bigr]=\frac{c^{-1}}{2}|\mu_{t}^{1,N}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t+\frac{c^{-1}}{{N}}\mathrm{d}t+\mathrm{d}\widetilde{K}_{t}^{1}+\mathrm{d}M_{t}^{1},

for all
t\in[t_{0}(\varepsilon),N^{1/4}\wedge\sigma_{N}(\varepsilon)\wedge\tau_{N}]
.
Next, for
t
in the same interval,

|\mu_{t}^{1,N}|^{2}\geq\exp\Bigl(\frac{c^{-1}}{2}t\Bigr)\biggl[\frac{c^{-1}}{{N}}\int_{t_{0}(\varepsilon)}^{t}\exp\bigl(-\frac{c^{-1}}{2}s\bigr)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t_{0}(\varepsilon)}^{t}\exp\bigl(-\frac{c^{-1}}{2}s\bigr)\mathrm{d}M_{s}^{1}\biggr].

In particular, for all
t\geq 0
,

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[{\mathbbm{1}}_{\{\tau_{N}>t_{0}(\varepsilon)\}}\bigl|\mu_{t_{0}(\varepsilon)\vee(t\wedge N^{1/4}\wedge\sigma_{N}(\varepsilon))}^{1,N}\bigr|^{2}\Bigr]\geq\frac{c^{-1}}{{N}}{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[{\mathbbm{1}}_{\{\tau_{N}>t_{0}(\varepsilon)\}}\int_{t_{0}(\varepsilon)}^{t_{0}(\varepsilon)\vee(t\wedge N^{1/4}\wedge\sigma_{N}(\varepsilon))}\exp\Bigl(\frac{c^{-1}}{2}(t-s)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\biggr].\end{split}

Choosing
t=N^{1/4}
, we obtain that

\begin{split}1&\geq\frac{2}{{N}}\exp\Bigl(\frac{c^{-1}}{2}\bigl(N^{1/4}-t_{0}(\varepsilon)\bigr)\Bigr){\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\bigl\{\sigma_{N}(\varepsilon)\geq N^{1/4},\tau_{N}>t_{0}(\varepsilon)\bigr\}\Bigr),\end{split}

at least if
N^{1/4}\geq t_{0}(\varepsilon)
, which yields

{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\bigl\{\sigma_{N}(\varepsilon)\geq N^{1/4},\tau_{N}>t_{0}(\varepsilon)\bigr\}\Bigr)\leq\frac{N}{2}\exp\Bigl(-\frac{c^{-1}}{2}\bigl(N^{1/4}-t_{0}(\varepsilon)\bigr)\Bigr).

Since
\tau_{N}\geq\sigma_{N}(\varepsilon)
if
\tau_{N}>t_{0}(\varepsilon)
, with
\eta=c^{-4}/4
in the definition of
\tau_{N}
, this completes the proof.
∎

5
Appendix

5.1
Regularization of real-valued functions defined on the space of probability measures

We state here
a regularisation result
for a function
\Phi
defined
on the space of probability measures on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
.
The
proof was introduced for the first time in the arXiv version v1 of this work, see

[
35
]
. The regularisation procedure has been then reexplained in deep in

[
23
]
. For this reason, we have just decided to give the main statement
but to omit the proof.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
\Phi:{\mathcal{P}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}

satisfy

(
Reg
-
\Phi
-(
{\alpha,2}
))
for
\alpha\in[0,1)
.
Then there exists a sequence of smooth functions
(\Phi_{n})_{n\geq 1}
,
satisfying

(
Reg
-
\Phi
-(
{4,3}
))

for each
n\geq 1
,
and converging to

\Phi
, uniformly on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
, such that
the bounds satisfied by

(\Phi_{n})_{n\geq 1}
in

(
Reg
-
\Phi
-(
{\alpha,2}
))

are uniform in
n\geq 1
.

5.2
Marginal regularity of transition densities of large SDEs

Lemma 5.2
.

Consider a collection of bounded drifts
(b^{i}:[0,\infty)\times({\mathbb{T}}^{d})^{n}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d})_{i=1,\cdots,n}
, for two integers

d
and
n
, together with the solution
{\bm{X}}_{t}=(X_{t}^{1},\cdots,X_{t}^{n})_{t\geq 0}
to the particle system

\mathrm{d}X_{t}^{i}=b^{i}(t,{\bm{X}}_{t})\mathrm{d}t+\mathrm{d}W_{t}^{i},\quad t\geq 0,

for some deterministic initial condition
{\bm{X}}_{0}=(X_{0}^{1},\cdots,X_{0}^{n})\in({\mathbb{T}}^{d})^{n}
. For
any
t>0
, denote
by
[{\mathbb{T}}^{d}]^{n}\ni{\bm{x}}=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\mapsto p_{t}({\bm{x}})=p_{t}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})

the density of
{\bm{X}}_{t}
.

Consider a bounded measurable function
\varphi:{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\ni(x,m)\mapsto\varphi(x,m)\in{\mathbb{R}}

that
satisfies the following two properties:

(i)

for any
x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
,
the function

m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\mapsto\varphi(x,m)
is differentiable w.r.t
m
;

(ii)
the function
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\ni(x,m,y)\mapsto[\delta\varphi/\delta m](x,m,y)
is bounded (so that

\varphi
is Lipschitz continuous in
m
w.r.t.
\textrm{\rm dist}_{\rm TV}
, uniformly in
x
).

Then, for any
{\rho}\in(0,1)
, there exists a constant
c_{{\rho}}
only depending on the parameters

d
,
\rho
and the quantity

\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\sup_{t\geq 0}\sup_{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\|b^{i}(t,\cdot,m)\|_{\infty}
such that,
for all
y\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}

\begin{split}&\biggl|\int_{[{\mathbb{T}}^{d}]^{n}}\varphi{\Bigl(}x_{1}+y,\bar{\mu}^{n{-(1)}}_{{\bm{x}}}{\Bigr)}\frac{p_{t}(x_{1}+y,\cdots,x_{n})-p_{t}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})}{|y|^{{\rho}}}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x_{n}\biggr|\\
&\leq c_{\rho}\Bigl(\frac{1}{1\wedge t^{{\rho}/2}}+t\Bigr)\Bigl(\sup_{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\|\varphi(\cdot,m)\|_{{0},\infty}+\sup_{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\bigl\|\frac{\delta\varphi}{\delta m}(\cdot,m,\cdot)\|_{{0},\infty}\Bigr),\end{split}

where, in the above left-hand side,

\bar{\mu}^{n{-(1)}}_{,{\bm{x}}}
is equal to
(n-1)^{-1}\sum_{i=2}^{n}\delta_{x_{i}}
.

For
z\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
and
i\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
,
we write
ze_{i}
for the element of
[{\mathbb{T}}^{d}]^{n}
whose coordinate
i
is equal to

z
and whose coordinates
j
, for
j\not=i
, are zero.
Moreover, for a fixed
y\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
, with
y\not=0
and for a function
\varphi
as in the statement, we let

\begin{split}&\widetilde{\varphi}({\bm{x}}):=\varphi(x_{1},\bar{\mu}^{n{-(1)}}_{{\bm{x}}}),\quad\Phi({\bm{x}}){:=}\frac{\widetilde{\varphi}({{\bm{x}}}+ye_{1})-\widetilde{\varphi}({{\bm{x}}})}{|y|^{{\rho}}},\quad{\bm{x}}=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\in\bigl[{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\bigr]^{n},\end{split}

For

i\in\{2,\cdots,n\}
and
z\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
, we have

(5.1)

\begin{split}\Phi({\bm{x}}+ze_{i})-\Phi({\bm{x}})&=\frac{\widetilde{\varphi}({\bm{x}}+ye_{1}+ze_{i})-\widetilde{\varphi}({\bm{x}}+ye_{1})}{|y|^{{\rho}}}-\frac{\widetilde{\varphi}({\bm{x}}+ze_{i})-\widetilde{\varphi}({\bm{x}})}{|y|^{{\rho}}}=\frac{\Psi({\bm{x}}+ye_{1},z)-\Psi({\bm{x}},z)}{|y|^{{\rho}}},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}&\Psi({\bm{x}},z)=\widetilde{\varphi}({\bm{x}}+ze_{i})-\widetilde{\varphi}({\bm{x}})=\varphi\Bigl(x_{1},\bar{\mu}^{n{-(1)}}_{{\bm{x}}}+\frac{1}{n-1}\bigl[\delta_{x_{i}+z}-\delta_{x_{i}}\bigr]\Bigr)-\varphi(x_{1},\bar{\mu}^{n{-(1)}}_{{\bm{x}}})\\
&=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}\lambda}{n-1}\Bigl[\frac{\delta\varphi}{\delta m}\Bigl(x_{1},\bar{\mu}^{n{-(1)}}_{{\bm{x}}}+\frac{\lambda}{n-1}\bigl[\delta_{x_{i}+z}-\delta_{x_{i}}\bigr]\Bigr)(x_{i}+z)-\frac{\delta\varphi}{\delta m}\Bigl(x_{1},\bar{\mu}^{n{-(1)}}_{{\bm{x}}}+\frac{\lambda}{n-1}\bigl[\delta_{x_{i}+z}-\delta_{x_{i}}\bigr]\Bigr)(x_{i})\Bigr].\end{split}

Next, we consider the PDE (in dimension
d\times n
)

\partial_{s}u(s,{\bm{x}})+\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{{\bm{x}}}u(s,{\bm{x}})=0,\quad\bigl(s,{\bm{x}}\bigr)\in[0,t]\times\bigl({\mathbb{T}}^{d}\bigr)^{n}\,;\quad u(t,{\bm{x}})=\Phi({\bm{x}}).

Obviously,

u(s,{\bm{x}})=\Phi*g^{(d\times n)}_{t-s}({\bm{x}})=\frac{1}{|y|^{{\rho}}}\bigl(\widetilde{\varphi}(\cdot+ye_{1})-\widetilde{\varphi}(\cdot)\bigr)*g_{t-s}^{(d\times n)}({\bm{x}})=\frac{1}{|y|^{{\rho}}}\bigl(\widetilde{\varphi}*g^{(d\times n)}_{t-s}\bigl({\bm{x}}+{ye_{1}}\bigr)-\widetilde{\varphi}*g_{t-s}^{(d\times n)}({\bm{x}})\bigr),

where
g_{r}^{(d\times n)}
is the usual Gaussian kernel at time
r>0
in dimension
d\times n
. And then,

(5.2)

\begin{split}&|u(s,{\bm{x}})|\leq\frac{c_{{\rho}}}{1\wedge(t-s)^{{\rho}/2}}\sup_{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\bigl\|\varphi(\cdot,m)\bigr\|_{0,\infty},\quad|\nabla_{x_{1}}u(s,{\bm{x}})|\leq\frac{c_{{\rho}}}{1\wedge(t-s)^{(1+{\rho})/2}}\sup_{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\bigl\|\varphi(\cdot,m)\bigr\|_{0,\infty},\end{split}

for a constant
c_{{\rho}}\geq 0
(only depending on
d
and
{\rho}
–and in particular, not depending on
n
–).
In order to provide a similar bound but for
the gradients w.r.t.
(x_{i})_{{2\leq i\leq n}}
, we use
(
5.1
)
which allows us to write

\begin{split}&u(s,{\bm{x}}+ze_{i})-u(s,{\bm{x}})=\frac{1}{|y|^{{\rho}}}\Psi(\cdot,z)*\Bigl(g^{(d\times n)}_{t-s}\bigl({\bm{x}}+ye_{1}\bigr)-g_{t-s}^{(d\times n)}({\bm{x}})\Bigr),\end{split}

which yields in turn:

\Bigl|\nabla_{x_{i}}\Bigl[u(s,{\bm{x}}+ze_{i})-u(s,{\bm{x}})\Bigr]\Bigr|\leq\frac{c_{\gamma}}{(n-1)[1\wedge(t-s)^{(1+{\rho})/2}]}\sup_{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\bigl\|\frac{\delta\varphi}{\delta m}(\cdot,m)(\cdot)\bigr\|_{{0},\infty},

for
i\in\{2,\cdots,n\}
.
Integrating in
z\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
,
we get

(5.3)

\Bigl|\nabla_{x_{i}}u(s,{\bm{x}})\Bigr|\leq\frac{c_{\rho}}{(n-1)(t-s)^{(1+{\rho})/2}}\sup_{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\bigl\|\frac{\delta\varphi}{\delta m}(\cdot,m)(\cdot)\bigr\|_{{0},\infty},

for
i\in\{2,\cdots,n\}
.
Now, we expand
{\mathbb{E}}[u(s,{\bm{X}}_{s})]
(w.r.t.
s
). We get

\begin{split}{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\Phi({\bm{X}}_{t})\bigr]=u(0,{\bm{X}}_{0})+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}b^{i}(s,{\bm{X}}_{s})\cdot\nabla_{x_{i}}u(s,{\bm{X}}_{s})\mathrm{d}s,\end{split}

and
then, using (
5.2
) together with the boundedness of
b
, we get

\Bigl|{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\Phi({\bm{X}}_{t})\bigr]\Bigr|\leq c_{\rho}\Bigl(\sup_{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\Bigl[\|\varphi(\cdot,m)\|_{{0},\infty}+\bigl\|\frac{\delta\varphi}{\delta m}(\cdot,m)(\cdot)\bigr\|_{{0},\infty}\Bigr]\Bigr)\Bigl(\frac{1}{1\wedge t^{{\rho}/2}}+t\Bigr).

Using a change of variable to rewrite
the above left-hand side in terms of the left-hand side appearing in the statement,
we get the conclusion.
∎
